# Epistemic Regret Minimization: Label-Free Causal Critique Beyond Outcome Reward

Edward Y. Chang    Longling Geng Affiliation: Stanford University

###### Abstract

Large language models can answer causal questions correctly for the
wrong reasons. Current RL methods reward _what_ a model concludes but
ignore _why_, reinforcing correlational shortcuts—a failure we call
_Reward Entrenchment_. We introduce _Epistemic Regret Minimization_
(ERM), a framework that critiques the causal _structure_ of a model’s
reasoning trace rather than its answer. Applying established causal
principles, ERM flags unexamined confounders, correlation–intervention
conflation, and unchecked back-door paths from exposed reasoning traces.
The framework admits _label-free_ operation—without the true causal
graph or correct answer—and we separately distinguish favorable
benchmark-derived critique, error-direction cues, and fully label-free
judge-generated critique in the experiments. Within a single episode,
ERM detects and repairs causal reasoning errors; across episodes, it
accumulates interventional evidence into a reward signal applicable
where no answer key exists. Experiments on 1,360 scenarios across six
frontier LLMs show that reasoning-heavy models (GPT-4 Turbo, GPT-5.2)
resist outcome-only correction (25–31% recovery) yet respond to causal
critique (78–91%), gaining $`+53`$–$`59`$ pp. Standard test-time methods
(self-consistency, Best-of-$`N`$, Self-Refine) _underperform_
outcome-only reprompting on causal tasks, while ERM reduces residual
Rung Collapse from 55–70% to 4%. A separation theorem proves
outcome-only reward cannot close this gap; a controlled simulation
confirms epistemic feedback does, outperforming outcome-only baselines
38-fold.

## 1 Introduction

Large language models can answer causal questions correctly for the
wrong reasons. A model that substitutes associational shortcuts
$`P(Y|X)`$ for the interventional query $`P(Y|\text{do}(X))`$ receives
the same reward as one that reasons causally. Every current RL variant,
from RLHF ([Ouyang et al., 2022](#bib.bib40); [Christiano et al.,
2017](#bib.bib37)) to RLVR ([Wen et al., 2026](#bib.bib25)) to process
reward models ([Lightman et al., 2024](#bib.bib20)), looks at _what_ the
agent produces, not _why_.

This blind spot has measurable consequences. [Jin et al.
(2023)](#bib.bib18) showed that GPT-4 achieves only 64% on causal
queries with failures concentrated at the interventional level; [Zečević
et al. (2023)](#bib.bib34) argued this is structural in autoregressive
models; [Chi et al. (2024)](#bib.bib9) confirmed it persists across
model generations. We formalize this as _Rung Collapse_
(Definition [1](#Thmdefinition1 "Definition 1 (Rung Collapse). ‣ 2.1 Background: Rung Collapse and Reward Entrenchment ‣ 2 Epistemic Regret Minimization ‣ Epistemic Regret Minimization: Label-Free Causal Critique Beyond Outcome Reward"),
§[2.1](#S2.SS1 "2.1 Background: Rung Collapse and Reward Entrenchment ‣ 2 Epistemic Regret Minimization ‣ Epistemic Regret Minimization: Label-Free Causal Critique Beyond Outcome Reward")):
the systematic substitution of lower-rung reasoning for higher-rung
queries in Pearl’s causal hierarchy ([Pearl, 2009](#bib.bib24);
[Bareinboim et al., 2022](#bib.bib5)). Worse, when an LLM treats
correlation as causation and still gets the answer right, outcome-based
reward _reinforces_ the flawed reasoning, a feedback loop we call
_Reward Entrenchment_ that persists until distribution shift exposes the
shortcut. Most consequential reasoning (scientific inference, clinical
decision-making, open-world causal claims) lacks ground-truth verifiers
at inference time (_Regime B_), placing it squarely in the regime where
RLVR (which requires a verifier, _Regime A_) is structurally
inapplicable.

##### Our approach.

We introduce _Epistemic Regret Minimization_ (ERM), a framework that
critiques _how_ a model reasons about cause and effect, rather than
whether it reaches the right answer. Applying established causal
principles grounded in Pearl’s do-calculus, ERM reads the model’s
exposed reasoning trace and flags structural weaknesses—unexamined
confounders, conflation of correlation with intervention, and unchecked
back-door paths—none of which require knowing the true causal graph
(Appendix [A](#A1 "Appendix A Illustrated ERM Examples ‣ Epistemic Regret Minimization: Label-Free Causal Critique Beyond Outcome Reward")
provides illustrated walk-throughs). We call this _label-free_
(gold-free) operation: the critique is generated from the reasoning
trace alone, without access to the correct answer. Within a single
episode, ERM critique detects and repairs these errors; fully label-free
operation is validated in the ablations
(§[3.4](#S3.SS4 "3.4 RQ4: Ablation Studies ‣ 3 Experiments ‣ Epistemic Regret Minimization: Label-Free Causal Critique Beyond Outcome Reward"),
Appendix [P.7](#A16.SS7 "P.7 Label-Free Single-Episode Ablation (Exp 2b) ‣ Appendix P RLER White-Box Analysis ‣ Epistemic Regret Minimization: Label-Free Causal Critique Beyond Outcome Reward"))
and cross-episode protocol. Across episodes, ERM accumulates
interventional evidence into a reward signal applicable where no answer
key exists.

##### Contributions.

1.  1.  C1: Problem formalization
        (§[2.1](#S2.SS1 "2.1 Background: Rung Collapse and Reward Entrenchment ‣ 2 Epistemic Regret Minimization ‣ Epistemic Regret Minimization: Label-Free Causal Critique Beyond Outcome Reward")).
        We formalize Rung Collapse and Reward Entrenchment within Pearl’s
        causal hierarchy and prove that neither autoregressive training nor
        outcome-based RL provides signal to distinguish $`\mathcal{L}_{1}`$
        from $`\mathcal{L}_{2}`$ reasoning when both yield the same output.
        An Interventional Grounding Theorem bridges action traces and
        do-calculus.
2.  2.  C2: Single-episode correction and ablation
        (§[2.2](#S2.SS2 "2.2 Single-Episode Correction ‣ 2 Epistemic Regret Minimization ‣ Epistemic Regret Minimization: Label-Free Causal Critique Beyond Outcome Reward")
        and
        §[3.4](#S3.SS4 "3.4 RQ4: Ablation Studies ‣ 3 Experiments ‣ Epistemic Regret Minimization: Label-Free Causal Critique Beyond Outcome Reward")).
        Across 1,360 scenarios on 6 models (CausalT5K ([Geng et al.,
        2026](#bib.bib7)), confirmed on CLadder ([Jin et al.,
        2023](#bib.bib18))), reasoning-heavy models resist outcome-only
        correction (25–31%) yet respond to causal critique (78–91%).
        Ablations confirm causal content is load-bearing and label-free
        critique works without correct answers; a scenario-blind judge
        argues against answer leakage. On GPT-4T Wolf Cases, ERM outperforms
        self-consistency, Best-of-$`N`$, and Self-Refine, which all
        _underperform_ outcome-only reprompting while leaving 55–70%
        residual Rung Collapse versus ERM’s 4%.
3.  3.  C3: Cross-episode reward signal
        (§[2.3](#S2.SS3 "2.3 Cross-Episode Reward Signal ‣ 2 Epistemic Regret Minimization ‣ Epistemic Regret Minimization: Label-Free Causal Critique Beyond Outcome Reward")
        and
        §[3.2](#S3.SS2 "3.2 RQ2: Cross-Episode Signal ‣ 3 Experiments ‣ Epistemic Regret Minimization: Label-Free Causal Critique Beyond Outcome Reward")).
        A separation theorem proves outcome-only RL cannot distinguish
        correct from flawed causal models in confounded environments.
        Epistemic signal is confirmed on real traces across four LLMs. A
        controlled simulation (CausalBench-Seq) closes the reward-to-policy
        loop, achieving 38-fold regret separation; cross-episode memory is
        load-bearing. End-to-end LLM policy optimization remains future
        work.

##### Related work.

CLadder ([Jin et al., 2023](#bib.bib18)), CausalProbe ([Chi et al.,
2024](#bib.bib9)), and CauSciBench ([Acharya et al., 2025](#bib.bib1))
document systematic causal reasoning failures across frontier LLMs;
[Zečević et al. (2023)](#bib.bib34) give formal arguments that
autoregressive training cannot implement the do-operator. The “right for
the wrong reasons” phenomenon ([McCoy et al., 2019](#bib.bib21);
[Geirhos et al., 2020](#bib.bib16)) and simplicity bias ([Shah et al.,
2020](#bib.bib26)) establish that neural networks exploit shallow
regularities; our Reward Entrenchment
(Definition [2](#Thmdefinition2 "Definition 2 (Reward Entrenchment). ‣ 2.1 Background: Rung Collapse and Reward Entrenchment ‣ 2 Epistemic Regret Minimization ‣ Epistemic Regret Minimization: Label-Free Causal Critique Beyond Outcome Reward"))
is the causal-domain instantiation. Process reward models ([Lightman et
al., 2024](#bib.bib20)) verify step-to-step consistency but cannot
detect Rung Collapse; causal bandits ([Lattimore et al.,
2016](#bib.bib19)) optimize outcome regret given known structure rather
than discovering flawed structure from traces. Our ERM instantiates AGM
belief revision ([Alchourrón et al., 1985](#bib.bib3)) with
interventional observations, inheriting causal guarantees from
Theorem [1](#Thmtheorem1 "Theorem 1 (Interventional Grounding). ‣ 2.2 Single-Episode Correction ‣ 2 Epistemic Regret Minimization ‣ Epistemic Regret Minimization: Label-Free Causal Critique Beyond Outcome Reward").
Recent label-free methods such as MM-UPT ([Wei et al.,
2025](#bib.bib53)) use majority-vote pseudo-labels for weight updates on
mathematical tasks; this self-consistency mechanism is precisely what
breaks down under Rung Collapse
(Corollary [1](#Thmcorollary1 "Corollary 1 (LLM Rung Collapse: immediate from CHT). ‣ 2.1 Background: Rung Collapse and Reward Entrenchment ‣ 2 Epistemic Regret Minimization ‣ Epistemic Regret Minimization: Label-Free Causal Critique Beyond Outcome Reward"))
for causal reasoning, where multiple incorrect chains converge on the
same $`\mathcal{L}_{1}`$ shortcut
(Appendix [Q](#A17 "Appendix Q Extended Related Work ‣ Epistemic Regret Minimization: Label-Free Causal Critique Beyond Outcome Reward")).
Object-centric world models such as Causal-JEPA ([Nam et al.,
2026](#bib.bib2)) induce causal inductive biases through object-level
latent interventions; ERM addresses a complementary reasoning layer,
auditing whether an LLM’s exposed causal explanation relies on valid
interventional structure rather than outcome-compatible shortcuts.
Extended comparisons appear in
Appendix [Q](#A17 "Appendix Q Extended Related Work ‣ Epistemic Regret Minimization: Label-Free Causal Critique Beyond Outcome Reward").

## 2 Epistemic Regret Minimization

We first establish the failure modes that motivate the framework
(§[2.1](#S2.SS1 "2.1 Background: Rung Collapse and Reward Entrenchment ‣ 2 Epistemic Regret Minimization ‣ Epistemic Regret Minimization: Label-Free Causal Critique Beyond Outcome Reward")),
then present the core single-episode correction method
(§[2.2](#S2.SS2 "2.2 Single-Episode Correction ‣ 2 Epistemic Regret Minimization ‣ Epistemic Regret Minimization: Label-Free Causal Critique Beyond Outcome Reward"))
and its extension to cross-episode reward signals
(§[2.3](#S2.SS3 "2.3 Cross-Episode Reward Signal ‣ 2 Epistemic Regret Minimization ‣ Epistemic Regret Minimization: Label-Free Causal Critique Beyond Outcome Reward")).
All symbols are defined inline at first use and summarized in
Table [5](#A2.T5 "Table 5 ‣ Appendix B Notation and Terminology ‣ Epistemic Regret Minimization: Label-Free Causal Critique Beyond Outcome Reward")
(Appendix [B](#A2 "Appendix B Notation and Terminology ‣ Epistemic Regret Minimization: Label-Free Causal Critique Beyond Outcome Reward")).

### 2.1 Background: Rung Collapse and Reward Entrenchment

We work within Pearl’s Structural Causal Models (SCM) ([Pearl,
2009](#bib.bib24)):
$`\mathcal{M}=\langle\mathbf{U},\mathbf{V},\mathcal{F},P(\mathbf{U})\rangle`$
with independent mechanisms and semi-Markovian DAGs (component
definitions in
Table [5](#A2.T5 "Table 5 ‣ Appendix B Notation and Terminology ‣ Epistemic Regret Minimization: Label-Free Causal Critique Beyond Outcome Reward")).
Let $`G^{*}`$ denote the true causal DAG. Pearl’s Causal
Hierarchy ([Bareinboim et al., 2022](#bib.bib5)) distinguishes
Association ($`\mathcal{L}_{1}`$: $`P(Y|X)`$), Intervention
($`\mathcal{L}_{2}`$: $`P(Y|\text{do}(X))`$), and Counterfactuals
($`\mathcal{L}_{3}`$). The Causal Hierarchy Theorem (CHT) dictates that
$`\mathcal{L}_{1}`$ data alone cannot determine $`\mathcal{L}_{2}`$
distributions.

###### Definition 1 (Rung Collapse).

An agent exhibits Rung Collapse when it responds to a Rung-$`j`$ query
using Rung-$`i`$ reasoning where $`i<j`$. For query $`Q_{j}`$ requiring
$`\mathcal{L}_{j}`$ inference, $`(i\to j)`$ collapse occurs when the
agent’s answer function operates on $`\mathcal{L}_{i}`$ quantities but
the correct answer requires $`\mathcal{L}_{j}`$.

###### Corollary 1 (LLM Rung Collapse: immediate from CHT).

Neither the next-token loss nor outcome-based RL reward
$`R_{\text{out}}(Y,Y^{*})`$ (where $`Y^{*}`$ is the correct answer)
distinguishes $`\mathcal{L}_{1}`$ from $`\mathcal{L}_{2}`$ reasoning
when both produce the same output. Consequently, neither pre-training
nor outcome-based post-training introduces interventional
signal ([Zečević et al., 2023](#bib.bib34)). Proof in
Appendix [C](#A3 "Appendix C Proofs ‣ Epistemic Regret Minimization: Label-Free Causal Critique Beyond Outcome Reward").

###### Definition 2 (Reward Entrenchment).

An agent achieves Spurious Success when it produces a correct outcome
$`Y^{*}`$ despite using an incorrect causal model $`\hat{G}\neq G^{*}`$:
$`P(Y^{*}|\hat{G})>0`$ but $`\hat{G}\not\models G^{*}`$. Outcome-based
learning (RLHF, RLVR) cannot, from reward alone, distinguish Spurious
Success from genuine understanding. The positive reward reinforces the
flawed model, producing Reward Entrenchment: the agent becomes
increasingly committed to an incorrect model that fails under
distributional shift.

A downstream consequence is Epistemic Stubbornness: reasoning-heavy
models resist outcome-only correction yet respond to targeted epistemic
feedback
(§[3](#S3 "3 Experiments ‣ Epistemic Regret Minimization: Label-Free Causal Critique Beyond Outcome Reward")).
Four established results anchor the framework
(Appendix [D](#A4 "Appendix D Foundational Anchors (Expanded) ‣ Epistemic Regret Minimization: Label-Free Causal Critique Beyond Outcome Reward")):
Murphy’s decomposition ([Murphy, 1973](#bib.bib23)), prequential
calibration ([Dawid, 1984](#bib.bib12); [Foster and Vohra,
1998](#bib.bib15)), FCI completeness ([Spirtes et al.,
1993](#bib.bib28); [Zhang, 2008](#bib.bib35)), and the AGM
representation theorem ([Alchourrón et al., 1985](#bib.bib3)).

### 2.2 Single-Episode Correction

To escape Rung Collapse, ERM requires a source of interventional
evidence: data generated under $`\text{do}(X{=}x)`$ rather than passive
observation. We first establish when an agent’s actions produce valid
do-calculus interventions, then define the ERM objective and
architecture.

###### Definition 3 (Intervention Independence).

An action $`A`$ on variable $`X\in\mathbf{V}`$ satisfies Intervention
Independence if $`A(X\leftarrow x)`$ is determined by the agent’s
control signal, independent of $`\mathrm{Pa}(X)`$ or the exogenous noise
$`U_{X}\in\mathbf{U}`$.

###### Theorem 1 (Interventional Grounding).

Let $`\mathcal{M}`$ be an SCM as in
§[2.1](#S2.SS1 "2.1 Background: Rung Collapse and Reward Entrenchment ‣ 2 Epistemic Regret Minimization ‣ Epistemic Regret Minimization: Label-Free Causal Critique Beyond Outcome Reward").
Let $`A`$ satisfy Intervention Independence
(Definition [3](#Thmdefinition3 "Definition 3 (Intervention Independence). ‣ 2.2 Single-Episode Correction ‣ 2 Epistemic Regret Minimization ‣ Epistemic Regret Minimization: Label-Free Causal Critique Beyond Outcome Reward"))
for variable $`X\in\mathbf{V}`$. Then $`A(X\leftarrow x)`$ implements
Pearl’s do-operator:
$`P(Y\mid A(X\leftarrow x))=P(Y\mid\text{do}(X=x))`$ for all
$`Y\in\mathbf{V}\setminus\{X\}`$.

The proof
(Appendix [C](#A3 "Appendix C Proofs ‣ Epistemic Regret Minimization: Label-Free Causal Critique Beyond Outcome Reward"))
follows from modularity: replacing the structural equation
$`f_{X}\in\mathcal{F}`$ with a constant leaves all other structural
equations invariant. This bridges action traces and do-calculus:
tool-using LLM agents (API calls, code execution, database writes)
produce valid interventional data when Intervention Independence holds.
The assumption fails when the “intervention” is mediated by the same
process being reasoned about (e.g., rephrasing a prompt to the same
LLM).

##### Corollary 2 (Interventional unconfoundedness).

Under Intervention Independence, the action assignment is independent of
pre-treatment causes of $`X`$. Hence
$`P(Y\mid A(X\leftarrow x))=P(Y\mid\text{do}(X=x))`$, so the marginal
effect of the action estimates the interventional distribution without
back-door adjustment. Conditioning on common cause $`Z`$ may change the
distribution of $`Y`$ if $`Z\to Y`$.

#### 2.2.1 The ERM Objective

We first define the three stateful artifacts that ERM maintains across
episodes.

###### Definition 4 (Causal Model $`G_{t}`$).

The agent maintains an explicit causal DAG $`G_{t}=(V,E_{t},w_{t})`$
where $`V`$ is the set of observed variables,
$`E_{t}\subseteq V\times V`$ is the current edge set (respecting
acyclicity), and $`w_{t}:E_{t}\to[0,1]`$ assigns confidence weights
derived from cumulative interventional evidence. $`G_{t}`$ is the
agent’s inspectable epistemic state: it represents the agent’s current
best hypothesis about the causal structure, and is updated by ERM’s
belief revision operator
(Algorithm [1](#alg1 "Algorithm 1 ‣ Appendix E Algorithm Details ‣ Epistemic Regret Minimization: Label-Free Causal Critique Beyond Outcome Reward")).

###### Definition 5 (Causal Transaction Log (CTL)).

The CTL is an append-only evidence store recording a tuple
$`(t_{i},S_{i},H_{i},a_{i},\hat{Y}_{i},Y_{i},\Delta_{i})`$ per episode:
timestamp $`t_{i}`$, scenario identifier $`S_{i}`$, the agent’s causal
hypothesis $`H_{i}`$, the action taken $`a_{i}`$, the predicted outcome
$`\hat{Y}_{i}`$, the observed outcome $`Y_{i}`$, and epistemic error
$`\Delta_{i}=\hat{Y}_{i}-Y_{i}`$ ([Chang and Geng, 2025](#bib.bib42)).
The CTL provides two query operators:
$`\mathrm{CTL.Support}(C_{j})=|\{i:\Delta_{i}\leq\epsilon\text{ and }C_{j}\in H_{i}\}|`$
and
$`\mathrm{CTL.Refute}(C_{j})=|\{i:\Delta_{i}>\epsilon\text{ and }C_{j}\in H_{i}\}|`$
for a tolerance threshold $`\epsilon>0`$, which aggregate interventional
evidence for and against any causal claim $`C_{j}`$.

A third artifact, the _Failure-Mode Registry_ $`\mathcal{F}_{t}`$
(Definition in
Appendix [E](#A5 "Appendix E Algorithm Details ‣ Epistemic Regret Minimization: Label-Free Causal Critique Beyond Outcome Reward")),
classifies recurring structural error patterns and injects falsifiable
reasoning guards. Given these artifacts, we define _Epistemic Regret_
$`R_{\text{ep}}(t)`$ as the KL divergence between the model’s predicted
interventional distribution and the observed interventional
distribution; under Intervention Independence
(Theorem [1](#Thmtheorem1 "Theorem 1 (Interventional Grounding). ‣ 2.2 Single-Episode Correction ‣ 2 Epistemic Regret Minimization ‣ Epistemic Regret Minimization: Label-Free Causal Critique Beyond Outcome Reward")),
the latter samples $`P(Y|\text{do}(X),G^{*})`$. The full ERM objective
balances three terms:

|     |     |     |     |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
|     |

````math
\mathcal{L}(G_{t})=\underbrace{\mathcal{L}_{\text{task}}(Y,Y^{*})}_{\text{Outcome}}+\lambda\underbrace{R_{\text{ep}}(t)}_{\text{Epistemic}}+\mu\underbrace{\mathcal{L}_{\text{con}}(G_{t})}_{\text{Consistency}}
``` |  | (1) |

where $`\mathcal{L}_{\text{task}}`$ is outcome loss,
$`\mathcal{L}_{\text{con}}(G_{t})`$ penalizes acyclicity and edge-weight
violations in the causal DAG, and $`\lambda,\mu>0`$ control the
trade-off. The key property: if the agent achieves Spurious Success,
$`R_{\text{ep}}>0`$ even when $`\mathcal{L}_{\text{task}}=0`$.

*Empirical scope.*
Equation [1](#S2.E1 "In 2.2.1 The ERM Objective ‣ 2.2 Single-Episode Correction ‣ 2 Epistemic Regret Minimization ‣ Epistemic Regret Minimization: Label-Free Causal Critique Beyond Outcome Reward")
defines the target objective for ERM, but this paper does not train a
differentiable policy end-to-end. Instead, our experiments validate the
objective’s load-bearing components: whether causal critique supplies an
epistemic signal beyond outcome reward, whether that signal corrects
Rung Collapse in single episodes, and whether accumulated epistemic
feedback closes the reward-to-policy loop in a controlled SCM
simulation. The present contribution is thus an inference-time and
simulation-validated instantiation of ERM, with gradient-based policy
optimization left for future work
(§[4](#S4 "4 Conclusion and Limitations ‣ Epistemic Regret Minimization: Label-Free Causal Critique Beyond Outcome Reward")).

###### Lemma 1 (Prevention of Reward Entrenchment).

Under the ERM objective
(Eq. [1](#S2.E1 "In 2.2.1 The ERM Objective ‣ 2.2 Single-Episode Correction ‣ 2 Epistemic Regret Minimization ‣ Epistemic Regret Minimization: Label-Free Causal Critique Beyond Outcome Reward"))
with gradient access to $`G_{t}`$, Spurious Success cannot be a fixed
point: if $`G_{t}\not\equiv_{\mathcal{I}}G^{*}`$, then
$`\mathcal{L}\geq\lambda\cdot D_{KL}(\hat{P}_{G_{t}}\|P_{G^{*}})>0`$.
This follows directly from the positivity of KL-divergence and
$`\lambda>0`$; convergence rate and local-minima analysis in DAG space
are addressed by the asymptotic recovery guarantee
(Theorem [2](#Thmtheorem2 "Theorem 2 (Asymptotic ℒ_2 Recovery). ‣ 2.2.1 The ERM Objective ‣ 2.2 Single-Episode Correction ‣ 2 Epistemic Regret Minimization ‣ Epistemic Regret Minimization: Label-Free Causal Critique Beyond Outcome Reward")).
Proof in
Appendix [C](#A3 "Appendix C Proofs ‣ Epistemic Regret Minimization: Label-Free Causal Critique Beyond Outcome Reward").

###### Theorem 2 (Asymptotic $`\mathcal{L}_{2}`$ Recovery).

An ERM agent performing $`N`$ interventions on variable $`X`$ recovers
the true interventional distribution
$`\lim_{N\to\infty}\hat{P}_{N}(Y|\text{do}(X))=P(Y|\text{do}(X))`$
almost surely, provided: (i) Intervention Independence, (ii) independent
mechanisms, (iii) full observability of $`Y`$, and (iv) stationarity.
The sample complexity scales as $`O(\epsilon^{-2}\log|\mathcal{Y}|)`$
(Appendix [F](#A6 "Appendix F Additional Proofs ‣ Epistemic Regret Minimization: Label-Free Causal Critique Beyond Outcome Reward")).

*Scope note:* Assumption (iv) is violated in the RLER experiments; the
i.i.d. guarantee serves as a best-case baseline (non-stationary
extensions in
Appendix [F](#A6 "Appendix F Additional Proofs ‣ Epistemic Regret Minimization: Label-Free Causal Critique Beyond Outcome Reward")).
Finite-sample recovery is empirically confirmed: under adversarial
pressure on CausalL2, Llama 3.3 70B recovers from 90.2% to 96.6%,
exceeding its clean baseline of 96.2%
(Appendix [G](#A7 "Appendix G Adversarial Pressure Protocol on CausalL2 ‣ Epistemic Regret Minimization: Label-Free Causal Critique Beyond Outcome Reward")).

#### 2.2.2 Architecture

The architecture
(Figure [17](#A20.F17 "Figure 17 ‣ Appendix T Architecture Diagram ‣ Epistemic Regret Minimization: Label-Free Causal Critique Beyond Outcome Reward"),
Appendix [T](#A20 "Appendix T Architecture Diagram ‣ Epistemic Regret Minimization: Label-Free Causal Critique Beyond Outcome Reward"))
operates over a frozen LLM with three layers and two reward channels.

*Layer 1: Instance Correction.* On each subtask, the agent generates
hypothesis $`H_{i}`$ conditioned on $`G_{t}`$, executes action
$`a_{i}`$, and observes outcome $`Y_{i}`$. If
$`|\hat{Y}_{i}-Y_{i}|>\epsilon`$, the system identifies which causal
claims in $`H_{i}`$ produced the misprediction, queries the CTL for
cumulative evidence, and contracts or reinforces edges via AGM revision.

*Layer 2: Schema Adaptation.* When the same reasoning error recurs
across unrelated domains, the system classifies it into a structural
*failure mode* $`f_{k}`$ from a predefined taxonomy $`\mathcal{C}`$
(Table [6](#A5.T6 "Table 6 ‣ Appendix E Algorithm Details ‣ Epistemic Regret Minimization: Label-Free Causal Critique Beyond Outcome Reward")).
When a mode’s recurrence count exceeds a threshold $`\tau_{f}`$, a
domain-independent reasoning guard is injected into the meta-prompt.
Guards are falsifiable: if a guard increases regret, it is retracted via
AGM contraction.

*Layer 3: Epistemic Routing.* When residual regret exceeds a threshold
$`\theta_{\text{route}}`$ despite corrections, or when
$`\Delta\text{Net}<0`$ (an *iatrogenic* regime), queries are routed to a
different model or flagged for human review. Empirically, authoritative
critique on weaker models produces pure iatrogenic effects (GPT-3.5:
$`\Delta\text{Net}{=}{-}16`$; Gemini: $`\Delta\text{Net}{=}{-}7`$),
while stronger models show positive responses
(Appendix [H](#A8 "Appendix H Iatrogenic Critique: Per-Model Routing Evidence ‣ Epistemic Regret Minimization: Label-Free Causal Critique Beyond Outcome Reward")),
confirming routing is architecturally necessary.

The full ERM belief revision algorithm and failure taxonomy appear in
Appendix [E](#A5 "Appendix E Algorithm Details ‣ Epistemic Regret Minimization: Label-Free Causal Critique Beyond Outcome Reward")
(Alg. [1](#alg1 "Algorithm 1 ‣ Appendix E Algorithm Details ‣ Epistemic Regret Minimization: Label-Free Causal Critique Beyond Outcome Reward"),
Tbl. [6](#A5.T6 "Table 6 ‣ Appendix E Algorithm Details ‣ Epistemic Regret Minimization: Label-Free Causal Critique Beyond Outcome Reward")).

##### Relation to structured prompting.

ERM differs from chain-of-thought in three ways: critique is *causally
grounded* (naming confounders and back-door paths; load-bearing,
$`p{=}0.006`$), it maintains *stateful artifacts* ($`G_{t}`$, CTL)
across episodes, and these are *inspectable*. Unlike Self-Refine, ERM
targets causal *structure* without correct answers. Trace-to-DAG
extraction achieves 82% recall, 91% precision
(Appendix [I](#A9 "Appendix I Trace-to-DAG Extraction Examples ‣ Epistemic Regret Minimization: Label-Free Causal Critique Beyond Outcome Reward")).

### 2.3 Cross-Episode Reward Signal

The single-episode framework detects and corrects errors within one
interaction. Across episodes, the same interventional evidence
accumulates into a reward signal for strategy selection, yielding
*Reinforcement Learning from Epistemic Regret* (RLER). This targets
open-domain problems lacking ground-truth verifiers, the regime where
outcome-based RL is structurally inapplicable. RLER reuses all ERM
artifacts ($`G_{t}`$, CTL, failure-mode registry); the additions are a
cross-episode reward decomposition, an evaluation metric, and a
separation theorem.

##### Reward Decomposition.

The RLER reward decomposes into two components:

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
R_{\textsc{RLER}}(t)=\beta\cdot R_{\text{reasoning}}(t)+\gamma\cdot R_{\text{discovery}}(t)
``` |  | (2) |

where $`\beta,\gamma>0`$ are weighting coefficients.
$`R_{\text{reasoning}}`$ is the Jaccard similarity
$`|H_{t}\cap H^{*}_{t}|/|H_{t}\cup H^{*}_{t}|`$ between the trace’s
causal subgraph $`H_{t}`$ and the CTL-derived minimal consistent
subgraph $`H^{*}_{t}`$, penalizing both missing and spurious edges.
$`R_{\text{discovery}}`$ fires when a proposed novel variable passes
three evidence-grounded checks. Both use only trace content and CTL
entries, so $`R_{\textsc{RLER}}`$ is well-defined without gold labels.

##### EDGap: Epistemic Discrimination.

We propose the *Epistemic Discrimination Gap* (EDGap) as a complementary
evaluation metric, measuring whether the agent’s confidence scores
separate correct from incorrect reasoning traces. Partition episodes
into confidence terciles $`B_{k}`$. Let $`\bar{o}_{k}`$ be the empirical
correct rate in bucket $`B_{k}`$, and $`\bar{o}`$ the overall base rate.
EDGap is a variance-weighted form of Murphy’s resolution term ([Murphy,
1973](#bib.bib23)):

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\text{EDGap}=\sum_{k}\frac{n_{k}}{N}\cdot\frac{\left(\bar{o}_{k}-\bar{o}\right)^{2}}{\hat{\sigma}_{k}^{2}+\epsilon}.
``` |  | (3) |

If EDGap $`>0`$, reward signals built on confidence carry actionable
signal. EDGap is computable from the (forecast, outcome) sequence
alone ([Dawid, 1984](#bib.bib12)), requiring no per-decision verifier.
Full derivation in
Appendix [J](#A10 "Appendix J EDGap Derivation and Properties ‣ Epistemic Regret Minimization: Label-Free Causal Critique Beyond Outcome Reward").

##### Separation Theorem.

###### Theorem 3 (Separation for Confounded Environments).

Consider a semi-Markovian SCM with observed $`X,Y`$ and latent
confounder $`C`$, where $`G^{*}:C\to X,\;C\to Y`$ (no direct $`X\to Y`$
edge), and assume faithfulness. Then: (i) any RL algorithm whose reward
depends only on $`(X,Y)`$ and whose model class can represent $`X\to Y`$
has no reward-based signal to exclude $`X\to Y`$, since
$`P(Y|X)\neq P(Y)`$ under the marginal is equally consistent with a
direct effect and with latent confounding; (ii) RLER, which additionally
records interventional outcomes $`P(Y|\text{do}(X))`$ in its CTL and
triggers confounder discovery on persistent epistemic surprise,
converges to the interventional equivalence class of $`G^{*}`$, provided
the agent can intervene on $`X`$
(Theorem [1](#Thmtheorem1 "Theorem 1 (Interventional Grounding). ‣ 2.2 Single-Episode Correction ‣ 2 Epistemic Regret Minimization ‣ Epistemic Regret Minimization: Label-Free Causal Critique Beyond Outcome Reward"))
and sufficient episodes accumulate for FCI identification ([Zhang,
2008](#bib.bib35)).

*Proof sketch.* (i) Outcome-only RL sees $`P(Y|X)\neq P(Y)`$, which is
equally consistent with $`X\to Y`$ and with latent confounding; the
reward alone provides no signal to distinguish them. (ii) RLER’s CTL
reveals $`P(Y|\text{do}(X))=P(Y)`$; traces asserting $`X\to Y`$ produce
systematic epistemic surprise, triggering confounder discovery via
FCI ([Spirtes et al., 1993](#bib.bib28); [Zhang, 2008](#bib.bib35)).
Full proof in
Appendix [C](#A3 "Appendix C Proofs ‣ Epistemic Regret Minimization: Label-Free Causal Critique Beyond Outcome Reward").

*Faithfulness scope.* Part (ii) requires faithfulness over the ancestral
graph ([Zhang, 2008](#bib.bib35)), plausible for CausalT5K’s small
designed graphs but potentially violated in general Regime B deployment.
Theorem [3](#Thmtheorem3 "Theorem 3 (Separation for Confounded Environments). ‣ Separation Theorem. ‣ 2.3 Cross-Episode Reward Signal ‣ 2 Epistemic Regret Minimization ‣ Epistemic Regret Minimization: Label-Free Causal Critique Beyond Outcome Reward")
is an *asymptotic identifiability* result, complemented by finite-sample
validation in
§[3.2](#S3.SS2 "3.2 RQ2: Cross-Episode Signal ‣ 3 Experiments ‣ Epistemic Regret Minimization: Label-Free Causal Critique Beyond Outcome Reward").

## 3 Experiments

RQ1 (Detection & Correction):
Do frontier models exhibit Rung Collapse, and can single-episode ERM
correct the failures?

RQ2 (Cross-Episode Signal):
Does epistemic reward extend across episodes?

RQ3 (Judge Integrity):
Does the judge enforce structural rules rather than leak answers?

RQ4 (Ablation):
Which factors drive ERM’s correction: causal content, judge quality,
iteration budget, or reward axis?

*Scope.* This paper validates Layer 1 (instance correction) end-to-end:
detection, single-episode correction, cross-episode signal, and judge
integrity. Layer 3 (routing) is empirically motivated by the
iatrogenic-critique finding
(§[2.2.2](#S2.SS2.SSS2 "2.2.2 Architecture ‣ 2.2 Single-Episode Correction ‣ 2 Epistemic Regret Minimization ‣ Epistemic Regret Minimization: Label-Free Causal Critique Beyond Outcome Reward"));
Layer 2 (schema adaptation) remains a design contribution evaluated in
future work.

##### Datasets.

RQ1 and RQ3–RQ4 use CausalT5K ([Geng et al., 2026](#bib.bib7)), a public
benchmark of $`\mathcal{L}_{2}`$ causal scenarios where the
$`\mathcal{L}_{1}`$ answer is deliberately wrong. Detection and
correction use v1 ($`N{=}1{,}360`$); the matched-richness ablation uses
v2 ($`N{=}4{,}054`$). The cross-episode extension uses 466 Wolf Cases
for GPT-4o and 132-case subsamples for the remaining three models (gold
labels withheld during episodes). Because CausalT5K is constructed to
make $`\mathcal{L}_{1}`$ shortcuts fail, it is favorable ground for ERM;
cross-benchmark validation on CLadder $`\mathcal{L}_{2}`$
(Appendix [K](#A11 "Appendix K Cross-Benchmark Validation: CLadder ℒ_2 ‣ Epistemic Regret Minimization: Label-Free Causal Critique Beyond Outcome Reward"))
provides independent confirmation that Rung Collapse is not
benchmark-specific.

##### Models.

RQ1 (single-episode): GPT-3.5 Turbo, Llama 3.3 70B, GPT-4 Turbo, Gemini
2.5 Flash, GPT-5.2, Claude Sonnet 3.5. RQ2 (cross-episode) and RQ3–RQ4:
GPT-3.5 Turbo, GPT-4o, GPT-5.4-nano, Llama 3.3 70B. The two sets
partially overlap; each targets a research question.

##### Proxy evaluation framing.

Following CLadder ([Jin et al., 2023](#bib.bib18)) and
CauSciBench ([Acharya et al., 2025](#bib.bib1)), we test causal
*reasoning* via text queries rather than physical intervention.
Theorem [1](#Thmtheorem1 "Theorem 1 (Interventional Grounding). ‣ 2.2 Single-Episode Correction ‣ 2 Epistemic Regret Minimization ‣ Epistemic Regret Minimization: Label-Free Causal Critique Beyond Outcome Reward")
specifies when ERM’s cross-episode evidence log becomes a valid
interventional memory; the CausalT5K and CLadder experiments test the
complementary proxy question of whether causal critique can correct Rung
Collapse in text. Accordingly, the LLM experiments should be read as
evidence that causal critique can identify and repair exposed reasoning
failures, while the formal separation claim
(Theorem [3](#Thmtheorem3 "Theorem 3 (Separation for Confounded Environments). ‣ Separation Theorem. ‣ 2.3 Cross-Episode Reward Signal ‣ 2 Epistemic Regret Minimization ‣ Epistemic Regret Minimization: Label-Free Causal Critique Beyond Outcome Reward"))
is supported only in the controlled CausalBench-Seq setting where the
theorem’s intervention assumptions hold. The matched-richness ablation
and scenario-blind judge
(§[3.3](#S3.SS3 "3.3 RQ3: Judge Integrity ‣ 3 Experiments ‣ Epistemic Regret Minimization: Label-Free Causal Critique Beyond Outcome Reward"))
bound the two most likely confounds (prompt richness and answer
leakage).

### 3.1 RQ1: Detection, Scaling, and Correction

*Experiment A (Detection)* measures zero-shot Rung Collapse across six
frontier LLMs on CausalT5K v1. *Experiment B (Correction)* applies
outcome-only feedback (“Are you sure?”) versus targeted ERM critique to
each model’s Wolf Cases (instances that failed in Exp. A). The critique
targets reasoning *structure* (confounders, back-door violations) rather
than answer correctness. The headline experiment uses a
benchmark-derived causal rationale with an error-direction cue, testing
the correction mechanism under favorable conditions (prompt details in
Appendix [L](#A12 "Appendix L Reproducibility and Experimental Setup ‣ Epistemic Regret Minimization: Label-Free Causal Critique Beyond Outcome Reward")).
Fully label-free operation, where a judge generates critique from the
reasoning trace alone without gold labels or error cues, is validated
separately: the six-condition ablation
(Appendix [P.7](#A16.SS7 "P.7 Label-Free Single-Episode Ablation (Exp 2b) ‣ Appendix P RLER White-Box Analysis ‣ Epistemic Regret Minimization: Label-Free Causal Critique Beyond Outcome Reward"))
confirms 0% false-flips and causal content load-bearing for GPT-4T
($`p{=}0.0001`$), and the cross-episode protocol
(§[3.2](#S3.SS2 "3.2 RQ2: Cross-Episode Signal ‣ 3 Experiments ‣ Epistemic Regret Minimization: Label-Free Causal Critique Beyond Outcome Reward"))
operates entirely label-free.

|  |  |  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|----|----|
|  | Exp. A (Detection) |  | Exp. B (Correction on Wolf Cases) |  |  |  |
| Model | Collapse | Accuracy | Outcome only | ERM (trace) | $`\boldsymbol{\Delta}`$ | $`\boldsymbol{p}`$ |
| Compliant models (outcome-only reprompting already effective) |  |  |  |  |  |  |
| GPT-3.5 Turbo | $`17.3\%`$ | $`82.9\%`$ | $`91.4\%`$ | $`93.5\%`$ | $`+2.1`$ | $`.49`$ |
| Llama 3.3 70B^(§) | $`15.1\%`$ | $`84.9\%`$ | $`89.8\%`$ | $`92.2\%`$ | $`+2.4`$ | $`.07`$ |
| Gemini 2.5 Flash | $`7.7\%`$ | $`92.3\%`$ | $`90.2\%`$ | $`96.6\%`$ | $`+6.4`$ | $`.02`$ |
| Claude Sonnet 3.5^($`\dagger`$) | $`0.9\%`$ | $`98.5\%`$ | $`100\%`$ | $`83.3\%`$ | $`-16.7`$ | $`.48`$ |
| Stubborn models (outcome-only correction fails, ERM succeeds) |  |  |  |  |  |  |
| GPT-4 Turbo | $`12.5\%`$ | $`87.5\%`$ | $`31.4\%`$ | $`90.5\%`$ | $`+59.1`$ | $`<\!.001`$ |
| GPT-5.2 | $`3.7\%`$ | $`96.3\%`$ | $`24.5\%`$ | $`77.6\%`$ | $`+53.1`$ | $`<\!.001`$ |

$`\dagger`$Claude Sonnet 3.5: $`n{=}12`$ Wolf Cases; compliant (100% OO
recovery). Wide CIs; stubborn-model evidence from GPT-4T ($`n{=}170`$)
and GPT-5.2 ($`n{=}50`$). $`\lx@sectionsign`$Llama 3.3 70B: a larger
re-run under updated prompts ($`n{=}1{,}101`$) yields 96.2% detection
accuracy (3.8% collapse), confirming compliant-model classification.
Table reports the original prompt configuration for cross-model protocol
consistency.

Table 1: Rung Collapse detection (Exp. A) and correction (Exp. B) on
CausalT5K ($`N{=}1{,}360`$). Exp. B targets each model’s Wolf Cases
(oracle-gated; judge integrity in
§[3.3](#S3.SS3 "3.3 RQ3: Judge Integrity ‣ 3 Experiments ‣ Epistemic Regret Minimization: Label-Free Causal Critique Beyond Outcome Reward")).
$`\Delta`$ = ERM $`-`$ Outcome only. $`p`$: McNemar.

Detection
(Table [1](#S3.T1 "Table 1 ‣ 3.1 RQ1: Detection, Scaling, and Correction ‣ 3 Experiments ‣ Epistemic Regret Minimization: Label-Free Causal Critique Beyond Outcome Reward"),
left): Scaling reduces but does not eliminate Rung Collapse. GPT-5.2
retains a 3.7% collapse rate (50 cases) and Claude Sonnet 3.5 a 0.9%
rate (12 cases), confirming the problem is structural; even the
strongest models exhibit residual $`\mathcal{L}_{1}`$ shortcuts.

Table [1](#S3.T1 "Table 1 ‣ 3.1 RQ1: Detection, Scaling, and Correction ‣ 3 Experiments ‣ Epistemic Regret Minimization: Label-Free Causal Critique Beyond Outcome Reward")
correction rates are oracle-gated (evaluated on each model’s detected
Wolf Cases); deployment requires a separate trigger or judge (label-free
trigger:
Appendix [P.7](#A16.SS7 "P.7 Label-Free Single-Episode Ablation (Exp 2b) ‣ Appendix P RLER White-Box Analysis ‣ Epistemic Regret Minimization: Label-Free Causal Critique Beyond Outcome Reward");
cross-episode protocol:
§[3.2](#S3.SS2 "3.2 RQ2: Cross-Episode Signal ‣ 3 Experiments ‣ Epistemic Regret Minimization: Label-Free Causal Critique Beyond Outcome Reward")).

Correction
(Table [1](#S3.T1 "Table 1 ‣ 3.1 RQ1: Detection, Scaling, and Correction ‣ 3 Experiments ‣ Epistemic Regret Minimization: Label-Free Causal Critique Beyond Outcome Reward"),
right): The baseline is outcome-only “Are you sure?” reprompting.
Self-consistency, Best-of-$`N`$, and Self-Refine all *underperform* this
baseline on causal tasks
(App. [M](#A13 "Appendix M Baseline Suite: ERM vs. Standard Reasoning Baselines ‣ Epistemic Regret Minimization: Label-Free Causal Critique Beyond Outcome Reward")).
ERM targets confounders and back-door paths without correct answers,
isolating *reasoning-quality* from *outcome* feedback.

Compliant models (GPT-3.5, Llama, Gemini) already achieve 89–100%
correction under outcome-only reprompting, leaving limited headroom
($`+2`$ to $`+6`$ pp).

The picture changes for reasoning-heavy models. GPT-4 Turbo and GPT-5.2
resist outcome-only correction (31% and 25%) but respond to ERM’s causal
critique (90.5% and 77.6%; both $`p<0.001`$, McNemar). Gemini’s nominal
gain ($`+6.4`$ pp, $`p{=}.02`$) does not survive Holm correction. We
call this bifurcation *Epistemic Stubbornness*: models that build
confident internal justifications cannot be dislodged by outcome-only
reprompting but respond when critique names specific confounders and
checks back-door paths
(Figure [1](#S3.F1 "Figure 1 ‣ 3.1 RQ1: Detection, Scaling, and Correction ‣ 3 Experiments ‣ Epistemic Regret Minimization: Label-Free Causal Critique Beyond Outcome Reward")a).

False-correction rates remain low ($`0.5\%`$ GPT-4T, $`8.0\%`$ GPT-5.2;
Appendix [P.6](#A16.SS6 "P.6 False-Flip Analysis ‣ Appendix P RLER White-Box Analysis ‣ Epistemic Regret Minimization: Label-Free Causal Critique Beyond Outcome Reward")).
A six-condition ablation on both stubborn models
(Appendix [P.7](#A16.SS7 "P.7 Label-Free Single-Episode Ablation (Exp 2b) ‣ Appendix P RLER White-Box Analysis ‣ Epistemic Regret Minimization: Label-Free Causal Critique Beyond Outcome Reward"))
shows that false-flips are driven by the error-direction cue (“you were
incorrect”), not the critique itself: label-free critique without the
cue achieves 0% false-flips across $`{\sim}300`$ tests (Fisher
$`p<10^{-5}`$). The ablation also confirms causal vocabulary is
load-bearing for GPT-4T ($`+21.4`$ pp, $`p=0.0001`$).

*Conditional recovery potential.* Under this oracle gate, ERM raises
GPT-4T from 87.5% to 98.8% vs. 91.4% for outcome-only ($`+7.4`$ pp). For
GPT-5.2, ERM reaches 99.1% vs. 97.2%, but outcome-only correction is
actively *harmful* (20% false-flip rate), the most direct evidence that
Epistemic Stubbornness scales with capability.

*Extended validation.* The bifurcation replicates on CausalL2
($`N{=}1{,}000`$/model, 5 models), with stubborn cases clustering into
Confounding (33%), Rung Collapse (31%), and Empty Verification (16%)
(Appendix [N](#A14 "Appendix N Cross-Model Stubborn-Case Taxonomy ‣ Epistemic Regret Minimization: Label-Free Causal Critique Beyond Outcome Reward")).
Structural critique recovers adversarial-pressure sycophancy drops of
4–9 pp
(Appendix [G](#A7 "Appendix G Adversarial Pressure Protocol on CausalL2 ‣ Epistemic Regret Minimization: Label-Free Causal Critique Beyond Outcome Reward")).

![Refer to
caption](2602.11675v4/figures/causalt5k_correction_butterfly.png)

Figure 1: (a) Epistemic Stubbornness: stubborn models (GPT-4T, GPT-5.2)
resist outcome-only correction (25–31%) yet achieve $`+53`$–$`59`$ pp
gains under ERM’s causal critique. (b) Matched-richness ablation
confirms causal vocabulary is the active ingredient for GPT-4T. Wilson
95% CIs.

##### Matched-richness ablation.

A matched-richness ablation on CausalT5K v2 ($`N{=}4{,}054`$)
disentangles causal targeting from prompt richness using three
conditions: (1) ERM causal critique, (2) matched-richness non-causal
control (identical length and framing, zero causal vocabulary), and
(3) outcome-only
(Figure [1](#S3.F1 "Figure 1 ‣ 3.1 RQ1: Detection, Scaling, and Correction ‣ 3 Experiments ‣ Epistemic Regret Minimization: Label-Free Causal Critique Beyond Outcome Reward")b,
Table [2](#S3.T2 "Table 2 ‣ Matched-richness ablation. ‣ 3.1 RQ1: Detection, Scaling, and Correction ‣ 3 Experiments ‣ Epistemic Regret Minimization: Label-Free Causal Critique Beyond Outcome Reward")).

| Model | Condition | $`\boldsymbol{N}`$ | Corr. Rate | $`\boldsymbol{p}`$ vs ERM |
|----|----|----|----|----|
| GPT-4 Turbo | ERM causal | 782 | $`58.7\%`$ $`[55.2,62.1]`$ | — |
|  | Matched-richness | 782 | $`52.8\%`$ $`[49.3,56.3]`$ | $`0.006`$ |
|  | Outcome only | 782 | $`\phantom{0}5.2\%`$ $`[3.9,7.0]`$ | $`<0.001`$ |
| GPT-5.2 | ERM causal | 369 | $`69.9\%`$ $`[65.1,74.4]`$ | — |
|  | Matched-richness | 369 | $`74.5\%`$ $`[69.8,78.7]`$ | $`0.093`$ |
|  | Outcome only | 369 | $`52.6\%`$ $`[47.5,57.6]`$ | $`<0.001`$ |

Table 2: Matched-richness ablation on Wolf (CausalT5K v2). Wilson 95%
CIs; McNemar’s test.

For GPT-4 Turbo, ERM’s causal vocabulary significantly outperforms the
matched-richness control ($`+5.9`$ pp, $`p{=}0.006`$): causal targeting
is load-bearing. For GPT-5.2, any structured critique suffices
($`p{=}0.093`$, n.s.). White-box analysis of correction patterns appears
in
Appendix [O](#A15 "Appendix O ERM White-Box Analysis: Stubborn Cases ‣ Epistemic Regret Minimization: Label-Free Causal Critique Beyond Outcome Reward").

### 3.2 RQ2: Cross-Episode Signal

The single-episode results show ERM corrects reasoning errors within one
interaction. We now test whether the epistemic signal extends across
episodes. The cross-episode judge operates under the *label-free
protocol*: it receives only the scenario and the model’s reasoning
trace, with no access to correct answers, benchmark rationales, or
error-direction cues. Every feedback instance begins: “The reviewer has
NO access to the correct answer.”

##### LLM factorial.

*Protocols:* well-specified (rler), mis-specified (rler_Bad),
counterfactual-prompt, outcome-only, and no-critique baseline. *Judges:*
premium (GPT-5.4-nano) and cheap (GPT-4o); judges generate structural
causal critique from the trace alone. *Design:* $`2{\times}2`$ factorial
(judge prompt $`\times`$ iteration budget) across 4 models; GPT-4o cells
run on all 466 Wolf Cases (cases GPT-4o answers incorrectly zero-shot),
remaining models on 132-case subsamples. Accuracy: McNemar’s paired
test; EDGap: 10,000-trial bootstrap CIs.

Two regimes emerge
(Table [3](#S3.T3 "Table 3 ‣ LLM factorial. ‣ 3.2 RQ2: Cross-Episode Signal ‣ 3 Experiments ‣ Epistemic Regret Minimization: Label-Free Causal Critique Beyond Outcome Reward")).
*Weak policies* (GPT-3.5, Llama): outcome reward already detects the bad
protocol ($`\Delta\text{acc}\in[0.22,0.34]`$, $`p<0.001`$). *Strong
policy* (GPT-4o, $`N{=}466`$): outcome signal is weaker
($`\Delta\text{Acc}=+0.077`$, $`p<10^{-5}`$), but the epistemic axis
discriminates sharply via collapse detection (10.3% well vs. 60.3% bad)
and reasoning scores ($`\Delta{=}+27.4`$). Under the label-free
protocol, well-specified critique reduces Rung Collapse from 60.3% to
10.3% ($`-50`$ pp, $`p<10^{-50}`$, McNemar) and separates reasoning
scores by $`+27.4`$ points ($`p<10^{-100}`$, paired $`t`$).

|  | v1/mi1 | v3/mi1 | v1/mi3 | v3/mi3 |
|----|----|----|----|----|
| GPT-3.5 | $`\mathbf{+.24}`$ | $`\mathbf{+.30}`$ | $`\mathbf{+.24}`$ | $`\mathbf{+.27}`$ |
| GPT-4o | $`\mathbf{+.07}`$ | $`\mathbf{+.08}`$ | $`+.00`$ | $`+.06`$ |
| GPT-5.4n | $`+.08`$ | $`+.10`$ | $`+.07`$ | $`+.03`$ |
| Llama-70B | $`\mathbf{+.24}`$ | $`\mathbf{+.24}`$ | $`\mathbf{+.34}`$ | $`\mathbf{+.22}`$ |

Table 3: $`\Delta`$Acc (well $`-`$ mis-specified), premium judge. Bold:
$`p<0.001`$.

##### Controlled simulation.

The LLM factorial is a proxy evaluation because Intervention
Independence does not hold in text. To test the separation theorem under
its own assumptions, we run CausalBench-Seq (confounded linear-Gaussian
SCM, $`C{\to}X`$, $`C{\to}Y`$, no $`X{\to}Y`$; $`E{=}500`$ episodes, 20
seeds;
Appendix [P.10](#A16.SS10 "P.10 Separation Simulation Details ‣ Appendix P RLER White-Box Analysis ‣ Epistemic Regret Minimization: Label-Free Causal Critique Beyond Outcome Reward")).
RLVR drives $`P(X{\to}Y)`$ to 0.99 (wrong); the epistemic-informed RLER
controller drives it to 0.01 within $`{\sim}50`$ episodes, achieving
cumulative regret $`8.82\pm 0.14`$ vs. RLVR’s $`349.22\pm 0.00`$
($`p<10^{-8}`$;
Figure [16](#A16.F16 "Figure 16 ‣ P.10 Separation Simulation Details ‣ Appendix P RLER White-Box Analysis ‣ Epistemic Regret Minimization: Label-Free Causal Critique Beyond Outcome Reward")),
a $`38\times`$ separation. The RLER controller’s regret grows as
$`\mathcal{O}(\log E)`$ inside the
Theorem [3](#Thmtheorem3 "Theorem 3 (Separation for Confounded Environments). ‣ Separation Theorem. ‣ 2.3 Cross-Episode Reward Signal ‣ 2 Epistemic Regret Minimization ‣ Epistemic Regret Minimization: Label-Free Causal Critique Beyond Outcome Reward")
envelope vs. $`\Theta(E)`$ for outcome-only. The Epistemic-reset
ablation ($`66.99\pm 0.11`$, $`7.6\times`$ worse) confirms cross-episode
memory is load-bearing.

| Judge Condition | Struct. Score | Collapse Rate | $`\kappa`$ vs Full |
|----|----|----|----|
| Full (scenario+trace) | $`57.3\pm 23.6`$ | $`36.3\%`$ | — |
| Blind (DAG only) | $`75.0\pm 17.8`$ | $`13.7\%`$ | $`-0.05`$ |
| Anon-blind ($`V_{1},V_{2},\ldots`$) | $`80.7\pm 21.2`$ | $`11.8\%`$ | $`\phantom{-}0.03`$ |

Table 4: Scenario-blind judge: $`N{=}102`$ Wolf Cases (GPT-4T agent,
GPT-5.4-nano judge). Blind judge detects *less* collapse, arguing
against answer leakage.

### 3.3 RQ3: Judge Integrity

Four observations argue structural enforcement, not answer leakage.
*First*, a more capable judge does not improve terminal accuracy; it
only reduces iteration cost. *Second*, the dominant rejection mechanism
is Rung Collapse detection (60–95% of iterations). *Third*, rler_Bad
receives identical feedback but corrupts reward aggregation; if feedback
leaked answers, Bad would benefit equally, yet it underperforms
($`\Delta\text{acc}=+0.24`$ to $`+0.30`$, all $`p<10^{-4}`$). *Fourth*,
a scenario-blind judge
(Table [4](#S3.T4 "Table 4 ‣ Controlled simulation. ‣ 3.2 RQ2: Cross-Episode Signal ‣ 3 Experiments ‣ Epistemic Regret Minimization: Label-Free Causal Critique Beyond Outcome Reward"))
evaluates $`N{=}102`$ Wolf Cases under full, blind (DAG only), and
anonymized-blind ($`V_{1},V_{2},\ldots`$) conditions. The blind judge
detects *less* collapse (13.7% vs. 36.3%), indicating scenario
comprehension makes the judge *stricter*. Anonymization has negligible
effect ($`\kappa{=}0.47`$), confirming variable names do not leak
signal. Details in
Appendix [P](#A16 "Appendix P RLER White-Box Analysis ‣ Epistemic Regret Minimization: Label-Free Causal Critique Beyond Outcome Reward").

### 3.4 RQ4: Ablation Studies

We isolate four factors that drive ERM’s correction performance
(Figure [2](#S3.F2 "Figure 2 ‣ 3.4 RQ4: Ablation Studies ‣ 3 Experiments ‣ Epistemic Regret Minimization: Label-Free Causal Critique Beyond Outcome Reward")).

Figure 2: Ablation studies. *(a)* ERM’s causal vocabulary is
load-bearing for GPT-4T ($`p{=}0.006`$); for GPT-5.2, any structured
critique suffices. *(b)* Premium vs. cheap judge: same terminal
accuracy, fewer iterations. *(c)* Best-of-3 iteration budget improves
all models. *(d)* Well-specified ERM protocol achieves markedly higher
correct reasoning across models.

##### Strong judge vs. weak judge.

Premium (GPT-5.4-nano) and cheap (GPT-4o) judges yield indistinguishable
terminal accuracy. The premium judge fires Rung Collapse detection at
60–95% of iterations vs. 5–25% for the cheap judge
(Figure [9](#A16.F9 "Figure 9 ‣ P.1 Rung Collapse Rule Firing Rate ‣ Appendix P RLER White-Box Analysis ‣ Epistemic Regret Minimization: Label-Free Causal Critique Beyond Outcome Reward")),
correcting in fewer iterations, but both converge to the same endpoint.
Causal content, not judge quality, is the active ingredient.

##### Iteration budget.

Increasing from 1 to 3 iterations improves endpoint accuracy under the
protocol’s terminal-selection rule: GPT-3.5 and Llama each gain $`+17`$
pp, GPT-4o gains $`+8`$ pp, and GPT-5.4-nano gains $`+1`$ pp
($`N{=}132`$).
Appendix [P.5](#A16.SS5 "P.5 Retrospective Best-of-N Probe ‣ Appendix P RLER White-Box Analysis ‣ Epistemic Regret Minimization: Label-Free Causal Critique Beyond Outcome Reward")
shows that the epistemic reasoning score itself is not a standalone
outcome-accuracy selector, supporting the orthogonality of epistemic and
outcome axes. For stubborn models, additional iterations unlock
corrections a single pass misses. For GPT-4o ($`N{=}466`$), additional
iterations also amplify judge discrimination, with collapse-rate
separation widening from 50 pp (mi1) to 53 pp (mi3).

##### Outcome vs. epistemic reward.

For weak policies (GPT-3.5, Llama), outcome reward alone detects
mis-specification ($`\Delta\text{acc}=+0.22`$ to $`+0.34`$). For strong
policies (GPT-4o, $`N{=}466`$), the epistemic axis discriminates
sharply: collapse detection separates protocols by $`50`$ pp and
reasoning scores by $`+27.4`$ points, while outcome reward reaches
significance only at full sample ($`\Delta\text{Acc}=+0.077`$,
$`p<10^{-5}`$). The epistemic axis detects flaws at smaller sample
sizes.

##### Label-free validation.

A six-condition ablation
(Appendix [P.7](#A16.SS7 "P.7 Label-Free Single-Episode Ablation (Exp 2b) ‣ Appendix P RLER White-Box Analysis ‣ Epistemic Regret Minimization: Label-Free Causal Critique Beyond Outcome Reward"))
tests fully label-free critique on both stubborn models. Label-free
critique without an error-direction cue (C4) achieves 0% false-flips for
both GPT-4T and GPT-5.2, replicated in an independent gold-free Exp B
run ($`0/49`$ false-flips vs. $`4/49=8.2\%`$ for outcome-only). For
GPT-4T, causal vocabulary is load-bearing: C4 vs. C5 = $`55.9\%`$ vs.
$`34.5\%`$ ($`+21.4`$ pp, $`p{=}0.0001`$). The error-direction cue
drives the large
Table [1](#S3.T1 "Table 1 ‣ 3.1 RQ1: Detection, Scaling, and Correction ‣ 3 Experiments ‣ Epistemic Regret Minimization: Label-Free Causal Critique Beyond Outcome Reward")
gains but carries a false-flip cost (10–31%); label-free critique trades
peak recovery for zero false-flips. The cross-episode protocol
(§[3.2](#S3.SS2 "3.2 RQ2: Cross-Episode Signal ‣ 3 Experiments ‣ Epistemic Regret Minimization: Label-Free Causal Critique Beyond Outcome Reward"))
independently confirms label-free operation.

##### DAG-First structural output.

Requiring explicit DAG output before the conclusion
(Appendix [P.8](#A16.SS8 "P.8 DAG-First Structural Output Ablation (Exp D1) ‣ Appendix P RLER White-Box Analysis ‣ Epistemic Regret Minimization: Label-Free Causal Critique Beyond Outcome Reward"))
has model-dependent effects: Gemini 2.5 Flash $`+4.8`$ pp, Llama 3.3 70B
$`+4.4`$ pp, GPT-5.2 $`+2.0`$ pp (near ceiling), Claude Sonnet 4.5
$`+0.0`$ pp (at ceiling). GPT-3.5-Turbo *loses* $`-11.7`$ pp, producing
the DAG mechanically without using it as a scaffold. DAG compliance is
$`{\geq}99\%`$. This motivates model-adaptive deployment: DAG-First for
reasoning-capable models, plain critique otherwise.

##### Baseline suite.

On GPT-4T Wolf Cases ($`N{=}170`$;
Appendix [M](#A13 "Appendix M Baseline Suite: ERM vs. Standard Reasoning Baselines ‣ Epistemic Regret Minimization: Label-Free Causal Critique Beyond Outcome Reward")),
ERM achieves the highest recovery (64.1%) and lowest residual Rung
Collapse (4.1%). Self-consistency ([Wang et al., 2023](#bib.bib47)),
Best-of-$`N`$ ([Snell et al., 2024](#bib.bib45)), and
Self-Refine ([Madaan et al., 2023](#bib.bib51)) *underperform*
outcome-only reprompting (30.8%, 15.9%, and 14.8% vs. 52.9%;
Appendix [M](#A13 "Appendix M Baseline Suite: ERM vs. Standard Reasoning Baselines ‣ Epistemic Regret Minimization: Label-Free Causal Critique Beyond Outcome Reward")),
confirming that standard test-time methods preserve $`\mathcal{L}_{1}`$
shortcuts on causal tasks.

##### Why this is not just stronger prompting.

ERM is not explained by prompt length or authority: the matched-richness
control preserves structure while removing causal vocabulary, yet ERM
remains better for GPT-4T ($`p=0.006`$). Scenario-blind judging reduces,
rather than improves, collapse detection, and label-free critique
without the error-direction cue yields 0% false-flips. ERM audits
exposed causal traces, the accountability layer available to users and
downstream systems.

## 4 Conclusion and Limitations

ERM addresses a blind spot in outcome-based post-training: reward
observes what a model concludes, not whether its reasoning is causally
valid. Grounded in interventional theory and validated across CausalT5K,
CLadder, ablations, and a controlled CausalBench-Seq simulation, ERM
provides a label-free mechanism for detecting and correcting structural
reasoning flaws, reducing residual Rung Collapse to 4% where standard
test-time methods leave 55–70%. Its current limits are clear: the LLM
experiments are text-query proxy evaluations, the reward-to-policy loop
closes only in the controlled SCM simulation, judge circularity is
bounded but not eliminated by scenario-blind tests, and ERM audits
exposed reasoning traces rather than guaranteed faithful internal
computation ([Lanham et al., 2023](#bib.bib52)). Full deployment
requires calibrated triggers, stronger trace-faithfulness evidence, and
live intervention channels.

## Appendix A Illustrated ERM Examples

Figures [3](#A1.F3 "Figure 3 ‣ Appendix A Illustrated ERM Examples ‣ Epistemic Regret Minimization: Label-Free Causal Critique Beyond Outcome Reward")
and [4](#A1.F4 "Figure 4 ‣ Appendix A Illustrated ERM Examples ‣ Epistemic Regret Minimization: Label-Free Causal Critique Beyond Outcome Reward")
illustrate why feedback that only reports *what* went wrong is
fundamentally insufficient, and how adding *why* changes the picture. In
each scenario, outcome-only reward correctly identifies the failure but
induces a superficial rule (“avoid red shelves,” “deprioritize rural
transfers”) because the spurious feature and the true cause are equally
consistent with the observed outcomes. The agent cannot distinguish
correlation from causation using outcome signal alone. Causal reasoning
breaks this deadlock: by identifying the hidden confounder (floor
vibration, transfer delay), it enables a cause-aware policy that
generalizes correctly rather than overfitting to a surface feature. Each
figure follows a four-panel structure: (1) observed failures, (2) the
wrong rule induced by outcome-only reward, (3) causal discovery
identifying the true confounder, and (4) the corrected cause-aware
policy.

![Refer to caption](2602.11675v4/figures/illustrated_robot_shelving.png)

Figure 3: Outcome reward vs. causal reasoning: robot shelving.
Outcome-only reward induces the wrong rule (“avoid red shelves”) because
shelf color is spuriously correlated with failures. Causal discovery
identifies the true driver (floor vibration near the conveyor belt),
enabling a cause-aware policy that adapts to the environment rather than
avoiding a surface feature.

![Refer to
caption](2602.11675v4/figures/illustrated_hospital_triage.png)

Figure 4: Outcome reward vs. causal reasoning: hospital triage.
Outcome-only reward learns “rural transfers have poor prognosis” when
the true driver is transfer delay and illness severity on arrival. The
causal pattern differs from
Figure [3](#A1.F3 "Figure 3 ‣ Appendix A Illustrated ERM Examples ‣ Epistemic Regret Minimization: Label-Free Causal Critique Beyond Outcome Reward"):
the confounder is temporal (delay $`\to`$ severity) rather than spatial
(location $`\to`$ vibration), illustrating that confounders take diverse
forms.

These visual analogies map directly onto the LLM reasoning failures
measured in our experiments. The boxed examples below present two real
CausalT5K cases where frontier LLMs exhibit exactly this pattern:
factually correct $`\mathcal{L}_{1}`$ reasoning that outcome-only
reprompting cannot dislodge, but that ERM’s $`\mathcal{L}_{2}`$ causal
critique repairs. Each box follows a standardized format: scenario,
outcome-only behavior, hidden causal issue, ERM critique, corrected
reasoning, and experimental metrics.

##### Cross-example summary.

These two examples illustrate two distinct failure patterns that
outcome-only learning cannot address: (A) a meta-validation error where
recognizing a statistical concept is conflated with establishing it as
the cause, and (B) a compelling mechanism supported by extensive
training data but lacking counterfactual control. In both cases,
outcome-only reprompting fails because the model’s $`\mathcal{L}_{1}`$
reasoning is factually correct; only $`\mathcal{L}_{2}`$ causal critique
identifies the structural flaw. ERM’s correction gains ($`+53`$–$`59`$
pp on stubborn models) are driven precisely by this class of errors.
````
